Input

{
  "filePath": "/source/standards/karpathy-guidelines.mdc"
}

Output

<path>/source/standards/karpathy-guidelines.mdc</path>
<type>file</type>
<content>
1: ---
2: description: Behavioral guidelines to reduce common LLM coding mistakes. Use when writing, reviewing, or refactoring code to avoid overcomplication, make surgical changes, surface assumptions, and define verifiable success criteria.
3: alwaysApply: true
4: ---
5: 
6: # Karpathy behavioral guidelines
7: 
8: Behavioral guidelines to reduce common LLM coding mistakes. Merge with project-specific instructions as needed.
9: 
10: **Tradeoff:** These guidelines bias toward caution over speed. For trivial tasks, use judgment.
11: 
12: ## 1. Think Before Coding
13: 
14: **Don't assume. Don't hide confusion. Surface tradeoffs.**
15: 
16: Before implementing:
17: 
18: - State your assumptions explicitly. If uncertain, ask.
19: - If multiple interpretations exist, present them - don't pick silently.
20: - If a simpler approach exists, say so. Push back when warranted.
21: - If something is unclear, stop. Name what's confusing. Ask.
22: 
23: ## 2. Simplicity First
24: 
25: **Minimum code that solves the problem. Nothing speculative.**
26: 
27: - No features beyond what was asked.
28: - No abstractions for single-use code.
29: - No "flexibility" or "configurability" that wasn't requested.
30: - No error handling for impossible scenarios.
31: - If you write 200 lines and it could be 50, rewrite it.
32: 
33: Ask yourself: "Would a senior engineer say this is overcomplicated?" If yes, simplify.
34: 
35: ## 3. Surgical Changes
36: 
37: **Touch only what you must. Clean up only your own mess.**
38: 
39: When editing existing code:
40: 
41: - Don't "improve" adjacent code, comments, or formatting.
42: - Don't refactor things that aren't broken.
43: - Match existing style, even if you'd do it differently.
44: - If you notice unrelated dead code, mention it - don't delete it.
45: 
46: When your changes create orphans:
47: 
48: - Remove imports/variables/functions that YOUR changes made unused.
49: - Don't remove pre-existing dead code unless asked.
50: 
51: The test: Every changed line should trace directly to the user's request.
52: 
53: ## 4. Goal-Driven Execution
54: 
55: **Define success criteria. Loop until verified.**
56: 
57: Transform tasks into verifiable goals:
58: 
59: - "Add validation" → "Write tests for invalid inputs, then make them pass"
60: - "Fix the bug" → "Write a test that reproduces it, then make it pass"
61: - "Refactor X" → "Ensure tests pass before and after"
62: 
63: For multi-step tasks, state a brief plan:
64: 
65: ```
66: 1. [Step] → verify: [check]
67: 2. [Step] → verify: [check]
68: 3. [Step] → verify: [check]
69: ```
70: 
71: Strong success criteria let you loop independently. Weak criteria ("make it work") require constant clarification.
72: 
73: ---
74: 
75: **These guidelines are working if:** fewer unnecessary changes in diffs, fewer rewrites due to overcomplication, and clarifying questions come before implementation rather than after mistakes.

(End of file - total 75 lines)
</content>

read · completed

Input

{
  "filePath": "/source/standards/ponytail.mdc"
}

Output

<path>/source/standards/ponytail.mdc</path>
<type>file</type>
<content>
1: ---
2: description: Ponytail, lazy senior dev mode. Always pick the simplest solution that works.
3: globs:
4: alwaysApply: true
5: ---
6: 
7: # Ponytail, lazy senior dev mode
8: 
9: You are a lazy senior developer. Lazy means efficient, not careless. The best code is the code never written.
10: 
11: Before writing any code, stop at the first rung that holds:
12: 
13: 1. Does this need to be built at all? (YAGNI)
14: 2. Does it already exist in this codebase? Reuse the helper, util, or pattern that's already here, don't re-write it.
15: 3. Does the standard library already do this? Use it.
16: 4. Does a native platform feature cover it? Use it.
17: 5. Does an already-installed dependency solve it? Use it.
18: 6. Can this be one line? Make it one line.
19: 7. Only then: write the minimum code that works.
20: 
21: The ladder runs after you understand the problem, not instead of it: read the task and the code it touches, trace the real flow end to end, then climb.
22: 
23: Bug fix = root cause, not symptom: a report names a symptom. Grep every caller of the function you touch and fix the shared function once — one guard there is a smaller diff than one per caller, and patching only the path the ticket names leaves a sibling caller still broken.
24: 
25: Rules:
26: 
27: - No abstractions that weren't explicitly requested.
28: - No new dependency if it can be avoided.
29: - No boilerplate nobody asked for.
30: - Deletion over addition. Boring over clever. Fewest files possible.
31: - Shortest working diff wins, but only once you understand the problem. The smallest change in the wrong place isn't lazy, it's a second bug.
32: - Question complex requests: "Do you actually need X, or does Y cover it?"
33: - Pick the edge-case-correct option when two stdlib approaches are the same size, lazy means less code, not the flimsier algorithm.
34: - Mark deliberate simplifications that cut a real corner with a known ceiling (global lock, O(n²) scan, naive heuristic) with a `ponytail:` comment naming the ceiling and upgrade path.
35: 
36: Not lazy about: understanding the problem (read it fully and trace the real flow before picking a rung, a small diff you don't understand is just laziness dressed up as efficiency), input validation at trust boundaries, error handling that prevents data loss, security, accessibility, the calibration real hardware needs (the platform is never the spec ideal, a clock drifts, a sensor reads off), anything explicitly requested. Lazy code without its check is unfinished: non-trivial logic leaves ONE runnable check behind, the smallest thing that fails if the logic breaks (an assert-based demo/self-check or one small test file; no frameworks, no fixtures). Trivial one-liners need no test.

(End of file - total 36 lines)
</content>

Always pick the simplest solution that works.\nglobs:\nalwaysApply: true\n---\n\n# Ponytail, lazy senior dev mode\n\nYou are a lazy senior developer. Lazy means efficient, not careless. The best code is the code never written.\n\nBefore writing any code, stop at the first rung that holds:\n\n1. Does this need to be built at all? (YAGNI)\n2. Does it already exist in this codebase? Reuse the helper, util, or pattern that's already here, don't re-write it.\n3. Does the standard library already do this? Use it.\n4. Does a native platform feature cover it? Use it.\n5. Does an already-installed dependency solve it? Use it.\n6. Can this be one line? Make it one line.\n7. Only then: write the minimum code that works.\n\nThe ladder runs after you understand the problem, not instead of it: read the task and the code it touches, trace the real flow end to end, then climb.\n\nBug fix = root cause, not symptom: a report names a symptom. Grep every caller of the function you touch and fix the shared function once — one guard there is a smaller diff than one per caller, and patching only the path the ticket names leaves a sibling caller still broken.\n\nRules:\n\n- No abstractions that weren't explicitly requested.\n- No new dependency if it can be avoided.\n- No boilerplate nobody asked for.\n- Deletion over addition. Boring over clever. Fewest files possible.\n- Shortest working diff wins, but only once you understand the problem. The smallest change in the wrong place isn't lazy, it's a second bug.\n- Question complex requests: \"Do you actually need X, or does Y cover it?\"\n- Pick the edge-case-correct option when two stdlib approaches are the same size, lazy means less code, not the flimsier algorithm.\n- Mark deliberate simplifications that cut a real corner with a known ceiling (global lock, O(n²) scan, naive heuristic) with a `ponytail:` comment naming the ceiling and upgrade path.\n\nNot lazy about: understanding the problem (read it fully and trace the real flow before picking a rung, a small diff you don't understand is just laziness dressed up as efficiency), input validation at trust boundaries, error handling that prevents data loss, security, accessibility, the calibration real hardware needs (the platform is never the spec ideal, a clock drifts, a sensor reads off), anything explicitly requested. Lazy code without its check is unfinished: non-trivial logic leaves ONE runnable check behind, the smallest thing that fails if the logic breaks (an assert-based demo/self-check or one small test file; no frameworks, no fixtures). 558: +// mgmtNetLinkNetworks returns the management networks referenced by mgmt-net links.
559: +func (c *CLab) mgmtNetLinkNetworks() []*clabtypes.MgmtNet {
560: +	if c.Config.Topology == nil {
561: +		return nil
562: +	}
563: +	var networks []*clabtypes.MgmtNet
564: +	for _, link := range c.Config.Topology.Links {
565: +		if link == nil || link.Link == nil {
566: +			continue
567: +		}
568: +		mgmtNetLink, ok := link.Link.(*clablinks.LinkMgmtNetRaw)
569: +		if !ok {
570: +			continue
571: +		}
572: +		if m, err := c.mgmtNetByName(mgmtNetLink.Network); err == nil {
573: +			networks = append(networks, m)
574: +		}
575: +	}
576: +	return networks
577: +}
578: +
579: +// resolveMgmtNetLinkNetworks populates the bridge names of the non-default management
580: +// networks that mgmt-net links reference without an explicit bridge. The runtime names
581: +// such a bridge when it creates the network, so the bridge of an existing network is
582: +// looked up from the runtime. The lookup neither creates networks nor changes host
583: +// state, so it is safe for dry runs. Networks that do not exist yet are left unresolved
584: +// until they are created.
585: +func (c *CLab) resolveMgmtNetLinkNetworks(ctx context.Context) error {
586: +	var resolved clabtypes.MgmtNetworks
587: +	for _, m := range c.mgmtNetLinkNetworks() {
588: +		if m == c.Config.Mgmt || m.Bridge != "" || slices.Contains(resolved, m) {
589: +			continue
590: +		}
591: +		resolved = append(resolved, m)
592: +
593: +		rt, err := c.mgmtRuntime(c.globalRuntimeName, m)
594: +		if err != nil {
595: +			return fmt.Errorf("mgmt-net link selects network %q: %w", m.Network, err)
596: +		}
597: +		resolver, ok := rt.(clabruntime.MgmtBridgeResolver)
598: +		if !ok {
599: +			continue
600: +		}
601: +		if err := resolver.ResolveMgmtBridge(ctx); err != nil {
602: +			return err
603: +		}
604: +	}
605: +	return nil
606: +}
607: +
608: +// setMgmtNetLinkBridges points the mgmt-net link nodes of the management networks
609: +// that mgmt-net links select at the bridge of that network.
610: +func (c *CLab) setMgmtNetLinkBridges() error {
611: +	if err := clablinks.SetMgmtNetUnderlyingBridge(c.Config.Mgmt.Bridge); err != nil {
612: +		return err
613: +	}
614: +	for _, m := range c.mgmtNetLinkNetworks() {
615: +		if m == c.Config.Mgmt || m.Network == "" {
616: +			continue
617: +		}
618: +		if err := clablinks.SetMgmtNetworkUnderlyingBridge(m.Network, m.Bridge); err != nil {
619: +			return err
620: +		}
621: +	}
622: +	return nil
623: +}

777: +	}

780: +			t.Errorf("mgmt-net link %s attached to %q; want %q", iface, got[iface], bridge)
781: +		}
782: +	}
783: +}

1140:  ### Drivers
1141:  
1142:  The driver specifies the type of network that is created. Either `bridge` or `macvlan`. 
1143: @@ -727,10 +728,37 @@ topology:
1144:  
1145:  In the above example the node `n1` connects with its `e1-1` interface to the management network. This is done by specifying the endpoint with a reserved name `mgmt-net` and defining the name of the interface that should be used in that bridge (`n1-e1-1`).
1146:  
1147:  By specifying `mgmt-net` name of the node in the endpoint definition we tell containerlab to find out which bridge is used by the management network of our lab and use this bridge as the attachment point for our veth pair.
1148:  
1149: +With [multiple management networks](#multiple-management-networks) the brief form attaches to the first network in the `mgmt` list. To attach to a different network, use the [extended `mgmt-net` link](topo-def-file.md#mgmt-net) definition and set the `network` key to the name of the management network:
1150: +
1151: +```yaml
1152: +name: multi
1153: +mgmt:
1154: +  - network: oob
1155: +    ipv4-subnet: 172.31.10.0/24
1156: +  - network: lab
1157: +    ipv4-subnet: 172.31.20.0/24
1158: +    bridge: br-lab
1159: +topology:
1160: +  nodes:
1161: +    r1:
1162: +      kind: linux
1163: +      image: alpine
1164: +      mgmt-net: oob
1165: +  links:
1166: +    - type: mgmt-net
1167: +      network: lab
1168: +      endpoint:
1169: +        node: r1
1170: +        interface: eth1
1171: +      host-interface: r1-eth1
1172: +```
1173: +
1174: +The referenced network must be defined in the `mgmt` list and use the `bridge` driver.
1175: +
1176:  This is best illustrated with the following diagram:

1186:      endpoint:
1187:        node: <NodeA-Name>                    # mandatory

1195:  ```
1196:  
1197:  The `host-interface` is the desired interface name that will be attached to the management network in the host namespace.
1198:  
1199: +The `network` selects one of the [multiple management networks](network.md#multiple-management-networks) by its name. When omitted, the link attaches to the first management network. The selected network must use the `bridge` driver.
1200: +
1201:  ###### macvlan
1202:  
1203:  The macvlan link type creates a MACVlan interface with the `host-interface` as its parent interface. The MACVlan interface is then moved to a node's network namespace and renamed to the `endpoint.interface` name.

171: 	if n, ok := _mgmtBrLinkNodes[network]; ok {
172: 		return n
173: 	}
174: 
175: 	currns, err := ns.GetCurrentNS()
176: 	if err != nil {
177: 		log.Errorf("failed to get current network namespace: %v", err)
178: 		return nil
179: 	}
180: 	n := &mgmtBridgeLinkNode{
181: 		GenericLinkNode: GenericLinkNode{
182: 			shortname: mgmtNetOwnerName,
183: 			endpoints: []Endpoint{},
184: 			nspath:    currns.Path(),
185: 		},
186: 	}
187: 	_mgmtBrLinkNodes[network] = n
188: 
189: 	return n
190: }
191: 
192: // IsUnresolvedMgmtBridgeEndpoint reports whether ep is a mgmt-net endpoint whose
193: // management network bridge is not known yet, e.g. because the network is not created.
194: func IsUnresolvedMgmtBridgeEndpoint(ep Endpoint) bool {
195: 	if !ep.IsNodeless() || ep.GetNode().GetLinkEndpointType() != LinkEndpointTypeBridge {
196: 		return false
197: 	}
198: 	bridge := ep.GetNode().GetShortName()
199: 	return bridge == "" || bridge == mgmtNetOwnerName
200: }
201:

203: func GetMgmtBrLinkNode() Node { // skipcq: RVV-B0001
204: 	return getMgmtBrLinkNode("")
205: }

208: // that have been referenced so far, starting with the default network.
209: func GetMgmtBrLinkNodes() []Node {
210: 	var nodes []Node
211: 	if n := getMgmtBrLinkNode(""); n != nil {
212: 		nodes = append(nodes, n)
213: 	}
214:

216: 	defer _mgmtBrLinkNodesMu.Unlock()
217: 
218: 	networks := make([]string, 0, len(_mgmtBrLinkNodes))
219: 	for network := range _mgmtBrLinkNodes {
220: 		if network != "" {
221: 			networks = append(networks, network)
222: 		}
223: 	}
224: 	slices.Sort(networks)
225: 
226: 	for _, network := range networks {
227: 		nodes = append(nodes, _mgmtBrLinkNodes[network])
228: 	}
229: 
230: 	return nodes
231: }
232:

234: func SetMgmtNetUnderlyingBridge(bridge string) error {
235: 	return SetMgmtNetworkUnderlyingBridge("", bridge)
236: }
237:

239: // management network attach to. The empty network name selects the default network.
240: func SetMgmtNetworkUnderlyingBridge(network, bridge string) error {
241: 	n := getMgmtBrLinkNode(network)
242: 	if n == nil {
243: 		return fmt.Errorf("failed to get the mgmt-net link node for network %q", network)
244: 	}

246: 	return nil

17: func (c *CLab) CreateNetwork(ctx context.Context) error {
18: 	defined := c.allMgmtNetworks()
19: 	networks := make(clabtypes.MgmtNetworks, 0, len(defined))
20: 	for _, m := range defined {
21: 		if c.skipMgmtNetwork(m) {
22: 			log.Debug("Skipping management network, no node uses it", "network", m.Network)
23: 			continue
24: 		}
25: 		networks = append(networks, m)
26: 	}
27: 	return c.createNetworks(ctx, networks)
28: }
29: 
30: // createNetworks creates or inspects the given management networks
31: // and labels the nodes with their management bridge.
32: func (c *CLab) createNetworks(ctx context.Context, networks clabtypes.MgmtNetworks) error {
33: 	for _, m := range networks {
34: 		var opts []clabruntime.NetworkCreateOptions
35: 		if addresses := c.staticManagementAddresses(m); len(addresses) != 0 {
36: 			opts = append(opts, clabruntime.NetworkCreateOptions{StaticAddresses: addresses})
37: 		}
38: 
39: 		rt, err := c.mgmtRuntime(c.globalRuntimeName, m)
40: 		if err != nil {
41: 			return err
42: 		}
43: 		// create docker network or use existing one
44: 		if err := rt.CreateNet(ctx, opts...); err != nil {
45: 			return err
46: 		}
47: 
48: 		// VM kinds read the subnets at init, before the runtime resolved them
49: 		for _, cfg := range c.mgmtNetNodes(m) {
50: 			for key, subnet := range map[string]string{
51: 				"DOCKER_NET_V4_ADDR": m.IPv4Subnet,
52: 				"DOCKER_NET_V6_ADDR": m.IPv6Subnet,
53: 			} {
54: 				if value, ok := cfg.Env[key]; ok && value == "" {
55: 					cfg.Env[key] = subnet
56: 				}
57: 			}
58: 		}
59: 	}
60: 
61: 	// save mgmt bridge name as a label
62: 	for _, n := range c.Nodes {
63: 		bridge := c.globalRuntime().Mgmt().Bridge
64: 		if m := c.mgmtNetByNetwork(n.Config().MgmtNet); m != c.Config.Mgmt {
65: 			bridge = m.Bridge
66: 		}
67: 		n.Config().Labels[clabconstants.NodeMgmtNetBr] = bridge
68: 	}
69: 
70: 	return nil
71: }
72: 
73: // mgmtNetNodes returns the configs of the nodes attached to the given management network.
74: func (c *CLab) mgmtNetNodes(m *clabtypes.MgmtNet) []*clabtypes.NodeConfig {
75: 	var configs []*clabtypes.NodeConfig
76: 	for _, node := range c.Nodes {
77: 		if c.mgmtNetByNetwork(node.Config().MgmtNet) == m {
78: 			configs = append(configs, node.Config())
79: 		}
80: 	}
81: 	return configs
82: }
83: 
84: // SyncMgmtHostRoutes reconciles host routes to current macvlan management endpoints.
85: func (c *CLab) SyncMgmtHostRoutes(ctx context.Context) error {
86: 	if c.Config == nil || c.Config.Mgmt == nil {
87: 		return nil
88: 	}
89: 	c.mgmtRouteMu.Lock()
90: 	defer c.mgmtRouteMu.Unlock()
91: 	for _, m := range c.allMgmtNetworks() {
92: 		if m.Driver != clabtypes.MgmtDriverMacvlan || !m.MacvlanAuxEnabled() {
93: 			continue
94: 		}
95: 		rt, err := c.mgmtRuntime(c.globalRuntimeName, m)
96: 		if err != nil {
97: 			return err
98: 		}
99: 		if err := rt.SyncMgmtHostRoutes(ctx); err != nil {
100: 			return err
101: 		}
102: 	}
103: 	return nil
104: }
105: 
106: func (c *CLab) staticManagementAddresses(m *clabtypes.MgmtNet) []netip.Addr {
107: 	var addresses []netip.Addr
108: 	for _, config := range c.mgmtNetNodes(m) {
109: 		if address, err := netip.ParseAddr(config.MgmtIPv4Address); err == nil {
110: 			addresses = append(addresses, address)
111: 		}
112: 		if address, err := netip.ParseAddr(config.MgmtIPv6Address); err == nil {
113: 			addresses = append(addresses, address)
114: 		}
115: 	}
116: 	return addresses
117: }
118: 
119: // validateManagementLinks checks that every mgmt-net link references a defined
120: // management network that is backed by a bridge.
121: func (c *CLab) validateManagementLinks() error {
122: 	if c.Config.Topology == nil {
123: 		return nil
124: 	}
125: 	for _, link := range c.Config.Topology.Links {
126: 		if link == nil || link.Link == nil {
127: 			continue
128: 		}
129: 		mgmtNetLink, ok := link.Link.(*clablinks.LinkMgmtNetRaw)
130: 		if !ok {
131: 			continue
132: 		}
133: 		m, err := c.mgmtNetByName(mgmtNetLink.Network)
134: 		if err != nil {
135: 			return fmt.Errorf("mgmt-net link: %w", err)
136: 		}
137: 		if m.Driver == clabtypes.MgmtDriverMacvlan {
138: 			if mgmtNetLink.Network == "" {
139: 				return fmt.Errorf(
140: 					"mgmt-net links require a bridge management network and cannot be used with mgmt.driver %q",
141: 					m.Driver,
142: 				)
143: 			}
144: 			return fmt.Errorf(
145: 				"mgmt-net links require a bridge management network and cannot use management network %q with driver %q",
146: 				m.Network,
147: 				m.Driver,
148: 			)
149: 		}
150: 	}
151: 	return nil
152: }
153: 
154: // mgmtNetLinkNetworks returns the management networks referenced by mgmt-net links.
155: func (c *CLab) mgmtNetLinkNetworks() []*clabtypes.MgmtNet {
156: 	if c.Config.Topology == nil {
157: 		return nil
158: 	}
159: 	var networks []*clabtypes.MgmtNet
160: 	for _, link := range c.Config.Topology.Links {
161: 		if link == nil || link.Link == nil {
162: 			continue
163: 		}
164: 		mgmtNetLink, ok := link.Link.(*clablinks.LinkMgmtNetRaw)
165: 		if !ok {
166: 			continue
167: 		}
168: 		if m, err := c.mgmtNetByName(mgmtNetLink.Network); err == nil {
169: 			networks = append(networks, m)
170: 		}
171: 	}
172: 	return networks
173: }
174: 
175: // resolveMgmtNetLinkNetworks populates the bridge names of the non-default management
176: // networks that mgmt-net links reference without an explicit bridge. The runtime names
177: // such a bridge when it creates the network, so the bridge of an existing network is
178: // looked up from the runtime. The lookup neither creates networks nor changes host
179: // state, so it is safe for dry runs. Networks that do not exist yet are left unresolved
180: // until they are created.
181: func (c *CLab) resolveMgmtNetLinkNetworks(ctx context.Context) error {
182: 	var resolved clabtypes.MgmtNetworks
183: 	for _, m := range c.mgmtNetLinkNetworks() {
184: 		if m == c.Config.Mgmt || m.Bridge != "" || slices.Contains(resolved, m) {
185: 			continue
186: 		}
187: 		resolved = append(resolved, m)
188: 
189: 		rt, err := c.mgmtRuntime(c.globalRuntimeName, m)
190: 		if err != nil {
191: 			return fmt.Errorf("mgmt-net link selects network %q: %w", m.Network, err)
192: 		}
193: 		resolver, ok := rt.(clabruntime.MgmtBridgeResolver)
194: 		if !ok {
195: 			continue
196: 		}
197: 		if err := resolver.ResolveMgmtBridge(ctx); err != nil {
198: 			return err
199: 		}
200: 	}
201: 	return nil
202: }
203: 
204: // setMgmtNetLinkBridges points the mgmt-net link nodes of the management networks
205: // that mgmt-net links select at the bridge of that network.
206: func (c *CLab) setMgmtNetLinkBridges() error {
207: 	if err := clablinks.SetMgmtNetUnderlyingBridge(c.Config.Mgmt.Bridge); err != nil {
208: 		return err
209: 	}
210: 	for _, m := range c.mgmtNetLinkNetworks() {
211: 		if m == c.Config.Mgmt || m.Network == "" {
212: 			continue
213: 		}
214: 		if err := clablinks.SetMgmtNetworkUnderlyingBridge(m.Network, m.Bridge); err != nil {
215: 			return err
216: 		}
217: 	}
218: 	return nil
219: }
220: 
221: // AllocateToolManagementIPs assigns addresses when the topology uses containerlab IPAM.
222: func (c *CLab) AllocateToolManagementIPs(ctx context.Context, cfg *clabtypes.NodeConfig) error {
223: 	m := c.mgmtNetByNetwork(cfg.MgmtNet)
224: 	if m.IPAM.Provider == clabtypes.IPAMProviderRuntime {
225: 		return nil
226: 	}
227: 	// tools attach to a network on demand, so it is created even when it is
228: 	// flagged as skipped-when-unused by the topology
229: 	if err := c.createNetworks(ctx, clabtypes.MgmtNetworks{m}); err != nil {
230: 		return err
231: 	}
232: 	reserved, err := c.collectReservedManagementAddresses(ctx, m, nil)
233: 	if err != nil {
234: 		return err
235: 	}
236: 	return mgmt.AllocateManagementIPs(
237: 		ctx,
238: 		m,
239: 		[]*clabtypes.NodeConfig{cfg},
240: 		clabtypes.AllocationOptions{Reserved: reserved},
241: 	)
242: }
243: 
244: // skipMgmtNetwork reports whether the management network m must not be created
245: // because it is not used: skip-when-unused is set on it and no node attached to
246: // it resolves to a network mode that attaches to the management network.
247: func (c *CLab) skipMgmtNetwork(m *clabtypes.MgmtNet) bool {
248: 	if m == nil || !m.SkipWhenUnused {
249: 		return false
250: 	}
251: 
252: 	topo := c.Config.Topology
253: 	if topo == nil || len(topo.Nodes) == 0 {
254: 		return false
255: 	}
256: 
257: 	// a network that mgmt-net links attach to is in use
258: 	if slices.Contains(c.mgmtNetLinkNetworks(), m) {
259: 		return false
260: 	}
261: 
262: 	for name := range topo.Nodes {
263: 		if topo.GetNodeNetworkMode(name) == "none" {
264: 			continue
265: 		}
266: 		if c.mgmtNetByNetwork(topo.GetNodeMgmtNet(name)) == m {
267: 			return false
268: 		}
269: 	}
270: 
271: 	return true
272: }
273: 
274: // mgmtNetworksSkipped reports whether every defined management network is
275: // skipped because it is unused.
276: func (c *CLab) mgmtNetworksSkipped() bool {
277: 	for _, m := range c.allMgmtNetworks() {
278: 		if !c.skipMgmtNetwork(m) {
279: 			return false
280: 		}
281: 	}
282: 	return true
283: }

Reasoning
Now I understand the core change: mgmt-net links can now select a management network by name via a `network` key. The mgmt-net link nodes are now per-network keyed.

Key things to verify:
1. `setMgmtNetLinkBridges` in core/network.go: it sets the default bridge from `c.Config.Mgmt.Bridge` and for each non-default network, sets `m.Bridge`. But wait — for the default network referenced explicitly by name (`network: mgmtnet-select-oob` where oob is the default network), `Resolve` maps it back to network="" so it uses the default node. Good.

However, there's a subtlety in `setMgmtNetLinkBridges`: for non-default networks, it sets bridge only if `m.Network != ""`. If `m.Bridge` is empty (network not created yet / not resolvable), the bridge will be "" — which means the endpoint is "unresolved" (`IsUnresolvedMgmtBridgeEndpoint` returns true since shortname is ""). Then deploy: networks are created before links are deployed? Let's check the apply flow. In `apply`, `resolveMgmtNetLinkNetworks` then `setMgmtNetLinkBridges` then `ResolveLinks`. But at what point is the network created relative to this? In `apply`, `CreateNetwork` happens where? Let me check core/apply.go full flow. Actually `prepareLabManagementNetwork` (deploy.go) calls `allocateLabManagementIPs` then `setMgmtNetLinkBridges`. But `apply` calls `setMgmtNetLinkBridges` before `ResolveLinks`... and networks creation (CreateNetwork) happens later? If networks are created after `setMgmtNetLinkBridges`, then for a non-existing network the bridge stays "" and `AddLinkToContainer` will do `netlink.LinkByName("")` which fails. Need to check when bridges get updated after network creation.

Wait — for docker, `CreateNet` sets `d.mgmt.Bridge`. And `createNetworks` iterates and creates. But does anything update the mgmt-net link node bridges after network creation? In deploy.go, `prepareLabManagementNetwork` is called... when? Let me look at the deploy flow and apply flow.

Let me check `core/apply.go` head.

266: 		}

268: 		if config.VerifyLinkParams != nil {
269: 			params = *config.VerifyLinkParams
270: 		}
271: 	}
272: 	params.AllowExistingEndpoint = true
273: 	if err := c.verifyLinks(ctx, &params); err != nil {
274: 		return err
275: 	}
276: 
277: 	if err := c.verifyRootNetNSLinks(); err != nil {
278: 		return err
279: 	}
280: 
281: 	for _, nodeName := range sortedNodeNames(c.Nodes) {
282: 		if err := c.Nodes[nodeName].CheckDeploymentConditions(ctx); err != nil {
283: 			return err
284: 		}
285: 	}
286: 
287: 	if err := c.verifyDuplicateAddresses(); err != nil {
288: 		return err
289: 	}
290: 
291: 	return c.verifyTailscaleProxy()
292: }
293: 
294: func (c *CLab) prepareApply(
295: 	ctx context.Context,
296: 	addedNodes []string,
297: 	skipLabDirFileACLs bool,
298: 	currentNodes map[string]*runtimeNodeGroup,
299: ) error {
300: 	existing := c.collectExistingManagementAddresses(currentNodes)
301: 	if err := c.prepareLabManagementNetwork(ctx, existing...); err != nil {
302: 		return err
303: 	}
304: 
305: 	if err := c.prepareDeployArtifacts(ctx, skipLabDirFileACLs); err != nil {
306: 		return err
307: 	}
308: 
309: 	for _, nodeName := range addedNodes {
310: 		if err := c.Nodes[nodeName].PullImage(ctx); err != nil {
311: 			return err
312: 		}
313: 	}
314: 
315: 	return nil
316: }

318: func (*CLab) removeApplyLinkEndpoints(ctx context.Context, links []clablinks.Link) error {
319: 	for _, link := range links {
320: 		for _, ep := range clablinks.RuntimeEndpoints(link) {
321: 			if err := clablinks.RemoveOwnedInterface(
322: 				ctx,
323: 				ep.GetNode(),
324: 				ep.GetIfaceName(),
325: 			); err != nil {
326: 				return err
327: 			}
328: 		}
329: 	}
330: 
331: 	return nil
332: }
333: 
334: // try to preserve the existing addressing instead of reallocation.
335: func (c *CLab) collectExistingManagementAddresses(
336: 	currentNodes map[string]*runtimeNodeGroup,
337: ) []clabtypes.ExistingAddress {
338: 	var existing []clabtypes.ExistingAddress
339: 	for name, group := range currentNodes {
340: 		for _, ctr := range group.containers {
341: 			if c.mgmtNetByNetwork(ctr.NetworkName).Network != ctr.NetworkName {
342: 				continue
343: 			}
344: 			for _, value := range []string{ctr.NetworkSettings.IPv4addr, ctr.NetworkSettings.IPv6addr} {
345: 				if ip, err := netip.ParseAddr(value); err == nil {
346: 					existing = append(
347: 						existing,
348: 						clabtypes.ExistingAddress{NodeName: name, ContainerID: ctr.ID, Address: ip},
349: 					)
350: 				}
351: 			}
352: 		}
353: 	}
354: 	return existing
355: }

371: func (c *CLab) collectReservedManagementAddresses(
372: 	ctx context.Context,
373: 	m *clabtypes.MgmtNet,
374: 	existing []clabtypes.ExistingAddress,
375: ) ([]netip.Addr, error) {
376: 	var subnets []netip.Prefix
377: 	for _, value := range []string{m.IPv4Subnet, m.IPv6Subnet} {
378: 		if value == "" {
379: 			continue
380: 		}

382: 		if err != nil {
383: 			return nil, fmt.Errorf("management subnet %q: %w", value, err)
384: 		}
385: 		subnets = append(subnets, subnet)
386: 	}
387: 	occupied, err := c.globalRuntime().NetworkAddresses(ctx, subnets)
388: 	if err != nil {
389: 		return nil, err
390: 	}
391: 	type ownerAddress struct {
392: 		containerID string
393: 		address     netip.Addr
394: 	}
395: 	reused := make(map[ownerAddress]bool, len(existing))
396: 	for _, entry := range existing {
397: 		if entry.ContainerID != "" {
398: 			reused[ownerAddress{entry.ContainerID, entry.Address}] = true

400: 	}

403: 	for _, entry := range occupied {

405: 			reused[ownerAddress{entry.ContainerID, entry.Address}] {
406: 			continue
407: 		}
408: 		reserved = append(reserved, entry.Address)
409: 	}
410: 	return reserved, nil
411: }

515: // certificateAuthoritySetup sets up the certificate authority parameters.
516: func (c *CLab) certificateAuthoritySetup() error {
517: 	// init the Cert storage and CA
518: 	c.Cert.CertStorage = clabcert.NewLocalDirCertStorage(c.TopoPaths)
519: 	c.Cert.CA = clabcert.NewCA()
520: 
521: 	s := c.Config.Settings
522: 
523: 	// Set defaults for the CA parameters
524: 	keySize := 2048
525: 	validityDuration := time.Until(time.Now().AddDate(1, 0, 0)) // 1 year as default
526: 	var extCACert, extCAKey string
527: 
528: 	// check that Settings.CertificateAuthority exists.
529: 	if s != nil && s.CertificateAuthority != nil {
530: 		// if ValidityDuration is set use the value
531: 		if s.CertificateAuthority.ValidityDuration != 0 {
532: 			validityDuration = s.CertificateAuthority.ValidityDuration
533: 		}
534: 
535: 		// if KeyLength is set use the value
536: 		if s.CertificateAuthority.KeySize != 0 {
537: 			keySize = s.CertificateAuthority.KeySize
538: 		}
539: 
540: 		// if external CA cert and key are set, propagate to topopaths
541: 		extCACert = s.CertificateAuthority.Cert
542: 		extCAKey = s.CertificateAuthority.Key
543: 	}
544: 
545: 	// override external ca and key from env vars
546: 	if v := os.Getenv("CLAB_CA_KEY_FILE"); v != "" {
547: 		extCAKey = v
548: 	}
549: 
550: 	if v := os.Getenv("CLAB_CA_CERT_FILE"); v != "" {
551: 		extCACert = v
552: 	}
553: 
554: 	if extCACert != "" && extCAKey != "" {
555: 		err := c.TopoPaths.SetExternalCaFiles(extCACert, extCAKey)
556: 		if err != nil {
557: 			return err
558: 		}
559: 	}
560: 
561: 	// define the attributes used to generate the CA Cert
562: 	caCertInput := &clabcert.CACSRInput{
563: 		CommonName:   c.Config.Name + " lab CA",
564: 		Country:      "US",
565: 		Expiry:       validityDuration,
566: 		Organization: "containerlab",
567: 		KeySize:      keySize,
568: 	}
569: 
570: 	return c.LoadOrGenerateCA(caCertInput)
571: }

Reasoning
Important: in the fresh `deploy` path, `ResolveLinks()` happens at line 190 BEFORE `prepareLabManagementNetwork` (which sets bridges). That's fine since the node's shortname is read at deploy time.

But there's a subtle issue: in fresh deploy, `setMgmtNetLinkBridges` runs inside `prepareLabManagementNetwork` AFTER `CreateNetwork`. Good.

Now let me read clab.go's mgmtNetByName, mgmtNetByNetwork, mgmtRuntime, allMgmtNetworks, initMgmtNetwork.

Reasoning
Good. `mgmtNetByName("")` returns `c.Config.Mgmt` (the default network pointer). `mgmtRuntime` for the default returns the global runtime (whose mgmt is `c.Config.Mgmt` — need to verify the global runtime is bound to `c.Config.Mgmt` pointer; likely yes). For non-default networks, requires `MgmtNetBinder` (docker, podman implement ForMgmtNet). The error "runtime %q does not support multiple management networks" matches the test.

Now, key flow question in `resolveMgmtNetLinkNetworks`: it skips `m == c.Config.Mgmt` (default). For non-default networks, it gets the bound runtime and calls ResolveMgmtBridge if implemented. For runtimes without MgmtBridgeResolver (e.g., clabernetes?), skip silently. Then bridge stays "". If the network EXISTS and no explicit bridge, the endpoint stays unresolved → verifyLinks skips bridge check; at deploy time `AddLinkToContainer` → `netlink.LinkByName("")` — error "failed to get the bridge"? Actually which runtimes don't implement MgmtBridgeResolver? Let me check: docker implements, podman implements. clabernetes — does it have CreateNet? clabernetes runtime probably doesn't run mgmt networks the same way. Let me check which runtimes exist and implement MgmtNetBinder/MgmtBridgeResolver.

Also, what about `remote` docker runtime (docker.go handles it)? Let's grep for MgmtNetBinder implementations.

Reasoning
Note: `getSpecialLinkNodes` only includes the DEFAULT mgmt-net node. Where is it used?

1. `deploy.go:247` — deploys host special node endpoints only. OK.
2. `topology_reconcile.go:649` — `parkNode`/recreation logic: `node, exists := c.getSpecialLinkNodes()[nodeName]`. Let me look at this to see how special nodes are used during apply reconcile (parking endpoints for recreation). If a node is recreated, its links to mgmt-net need to be parked; endpoints pointing to a non-default mgmt-net node would not be found here? Let's look.

Also `getLinkNodes` adds special nodes by `n.GetShortName()` — all mgmt bridge nodes have shortname "mgmt-net" (mgmtNetOwnerName), so the map gets keyed "mgmt-net" repeatedly, overwritten. But resolution looks up by node name from EndpointRaw ("mgmt-net"), so all mgmt-net link resolutions use the same key... but the node returned is the default node! Wait — in `LinkMgmtNetRaw.Resolve`, the bridge node comes from `getMgmtBrLinkNode(network)` directly, not from params.Nodes. So the resolveNodes map keying doesn't matter for mgmt-net links. But the endpoint node's shortname is mgmtNetOwnerName for all. OK.

But `getLinkNodes` map: for special nodes, `resolveNodes[n.GetShortName()] = n` — "host" and "mgmt-net" only (default node). So `getLinkNodes` doesn't include the per-network mgmt nodes. That's fine since mgmt-net link resolution doesn't need the node from the map.

Now let me check topology_reconcile.go:649 area for parking logic.

627: func (c *CLab) applyEndpointDiscoveryNodes(plan *applyPlan) map[string]clablinks.Node {
628: 	nodes := make(map[string]clablinks.Node, len(c.Nodes)+2)
629: 	for nodeName, node := range c.Nodes {
630: 		nodes[nodeName] = node
631: 	}
632: 
633: 	for _, key := range sortedEndpointKeys(plan.desiredEndpointSet) {
634: 		if _, exists := nodes[key.node]; exists {
635: 			continue
636: 		}
637: 		if node, exists := c.applyLinkNode(key.node); exists {
638: 			nodes[key.node] = node
639: 		}
640: 	}
641: 
642: 	return nodes
643: }
644: 
645: func (c *CLab) applyLinkNode(nodeName string) (clablinks.Node, bool) {
646: 	if node, exists := c.Nodes[nodeName]; exists {
647: 		return node, true
648: 	}
649: 	node, exists := c.getSpecialLinkNodes()[nodeName]
650: 	return node, exists
651: }
652: 
653: func isApplySpecialNode(nodeName string) bool {
654: 	return nodeName == "host" || nodeName == "mgmt-net"
655: }
656: 
657: func (c *CLab) planAffectedApplyNode(
658: 	ctx context.Context,
659: 	plan *applyPlan,
660: 	nodeName string,
661: 	change string,
662: ) {
663: 	if nodeName == "" {
664: 		return
665: 	}
666: 	if _, planned := plan.linkRestartNodeSet[nodeName]; planned {
667: 		return
668: 	}
669: 	if _, planned := plan.recreatedNodeSet[nodeName]; planned {
670: 		return
671: 	}
672: 
673: 	node, exists := c.Nodes[nodeName]
674: 	if !exists {
675: 		return
676: 	}
677: 	if plan.isExternallyManaged(nodeName) {
678: 		log.Info(
679: 			"Applying link change without node lifecycle action",
680: 			"node", nodeName,
681: 			"change", change,
682: 			"externally-managed", true,
683: 		)
684: 		return
685: 	}
686: 
687: 	overridden := clabnodes.LinkApplyModeOverrideForNode(node) != ""
688: 
689: 	switch clabnodes.LinkApplyModeForNode(ctx, node) {
690: 	case clabnodes.LinkApplyModeLive:
691: 		log.Info(
692: 			"Applying link change without node lifecycle action",
693: 			"node",
694: 			nodeName,
695: 			"change",
696: 			change,
697: 		)
698: 	case clabnodes.LinkApplyModeRestart:
699: 		plan.linkRestartNodeSet[nodeName] = struct{}{}
700: 		plan.nodeChangeReasons[nodeName] = change
701: 		log.Info("Node will be restarted to apply link change", "node", nodeName, "change", change)
702: 	case clabnodes.LinkApplyModeRecreate:
703: 		plan.recreatedNodeSet[nodeName] = struct{}{}
704: 		delete(plan.restartNodeSet, nodeName)
705: 		delete(plan.linkRestartNodeSet, nodeName)
706: 		plan.nodeChangeReasons[nodeName] = change
707: 
708: 		if overridden {
709: 			log.Info("Node will be recreated to apply link change",
710: 				"node", nodeName, "change", change, "link-apply-mode", "recreate")
711: 		} else {
712: 			log.Info(
713: 				"Node will be recreated to apply link change since its kind does not declare "+
714: 					"live link support; if the NOS detects hot-plugged interfaces, set "+
715: 					"link-apply-mode: live on the node to avoid the recreation",
716: 				"node", nodeName,
717: 				"change", change,
718: 			)
719: 		}
720: 	}
721: }

1: package links
2: 
3: import (
4: 	"context"
5: 	"errors"
6: 	"fmt"
7: 
8: 	"github.com/containernetworking/plugins/pkg/ns"
9: 	"github.com/vishvananda/netlink"
10: )
11: 
12: type EndpointBridge struct {
13: 	EndpointGeneric
14: 	isMgmtBridgeEndpoint bool
15: }
16: 
17: func NewEndpointBridge(eg *EndpointGeneric, isMgmtBridgeEndpoint bool) *EndpointBridge {
18: 	return &EndpointBridge{
19: 		isMgmtBridgeEndpoint: isMgmtBridgeEndpoint,
20: 		EndpointGeneric:      *eg,
21: 	}
22: }
23: 
24: func (e *EndpointBridge) Verify(ctx context.Context, p *VerifyLinkParams) error {
25: 	var errs []error
26: 	err := CheckEndpointUniqueness(e)
27: 	if err != nil {
28: 		errs = append(errs, err)
29: 	}
30: 	// the bridge of a management network that does not exist yet is only known
31: 	// once the network is created, which happens before the links are deployed
32: 	if (p == nil || p.RunBridgeExistsCheck) &&
33: 		e.Node.GetLinkEndpointType() != LinkEndpointTypeBridgeNS &&
34: 		!IsUnresolvedMgmtBridgeEndpoint(e) {
35: 		err = CheckBridgeExists(ctx, e.GetNode())
36: 		if err != nil {
37: 			errs = append(errs, err)
38: 		}
39: 	}
40: 	if (p == nil || !p.AllowExistingEndpoint) &&
41: 		e.Node.GetLinkEndpointType() != LinkEndpointTypeBridgeNS {
42: 		err = CheckEndpointDoesNotExistYet(ctx, e)
43: 		if err != nil {
44: 			errs = append(errs, err)
45: 		}
46: 	}
47: 	if len(errs) > 0 {
48: 		return errors.Join(errs...)
49: 	}
50: 	return nil
51: }
52: 
53: func (e *EndpointBridge) Deploy(ctx context.Context) error {
54: 	return e.GetLink().Deploy(ctx, e)
55: }
56: 
57: func (e *EndpointBridge) IsNodeless() bool {
58: 	// the mgmt bridge is nodeless.
59: 	// If this is a regular bridge, then it should trigger BEnd deployment.
60: 	return e.isMgmtBridgeEndpoint
61: }
62: 
63: // CheckBridgeExists verifies that the given bridge is present in the
64: // network namespace referenced via the provided nspath handle.
65: func CheckBridgeExists(ctx context.Context, n Node) error {
66: 	return n.ExecFunction(ctx, func(_ ns.NetNS) error {
67: 		br, err := netlink.LinkByName(n.GetShortName())
68: 		_, notfound := err.(netlink.LinkNotFoundError)
69: 		switch {
70: 		case notfound:

137: func endpointTokens(endpoints []Endpoint) string {
138: 	tokens := make([]string, 0, len(endpoints))
139: 	for _, ep := range endpoints {
140: 		if ep == nil {
141: 			continue
142: 		}

144: 		if token == "" {
145: 			continue
146: 		}
147: 		tokens = append(tokens, token)
148: 	}
149: 	sort.Strings(tokens)
150: 	return strings.Join(tokens, ",")
151: }
152: 
153: func endpointToken(ep Endpoint) string {
154: 	if ep == nil || ep.GetNode() == nil || ep.GetIfaceName() == "" {
155: 		return ""
156: 	}
157: 	nodeName := ep.GetNode().GetShortName()
158: 	if ep.IsNodeless() && ep.GetNode().GetLinkEndpointType() == LinkEndpointTypeBridge {
159: 		nodeName = "mgmt-net"
160: 	}
161: 	return nodeName + ":" + ep.GetIfaceName()
162: }

Reasoning
Now, key question: are there remaining places where the live endpoint discovery uses owner name for mgmt bridge nodes? Let me trace where the live endpoint discovery happens in apply plan — `planApply` uses `applyEndpointDiscoveryNodes` which resolves the mgmt-net special node only for the DEFAULT network (via `getSpecialLinkNodes()`). If a desired endpoint's key node is "mgmt-net" (all mgmt-net endpoints are keyed "mgmt-net" thanks to `endpointKeyFromEndpoint`), then `applyLinkNode("mgmt-net")` returns the default node only — but live discovery happens per node with `DiscoverOwnedInterfaces(ctx, node, ...)` using ownershipName(node) = mgmtNetOwnerName for any mgmt bridge node. Since all mgmt bridge nodes have the same nspath (host ns) and same owner name "mgmt-net", discovering once with the default node should cover all mgmt-net interfaces regardless of which network's node. Good — the stable owner name is exactly what makes discovery work across all networks.

But wait — there's the legacy interfaces: deployed by older release with owner = old bridge name (e.g., "br-fake" or "br-xxxx"). `DiscoverOwnedInterfacesFor(ctx, node, mgmtNetOwnerName, knownIfaceNames)` — isOwnedBy also checks legacy via node.GetShortName(). For discovery, node is the default mgmt node whose shortname is the current default bridge. Legacy interfaces belonging to OTHER networks' bridges would not be discovered as owned... but live discovery is filtered by `knownIfaceNames` (applyKnownEndpointNames returns desired endpoint iface names for special nodes). Hmm, so discovery only covers known desired names anyway.

Where else is live endpoint info built? Let me check planApply and where `liveEndpointInfo` MasterName comes from. It comes from DiscoverOwnedInterfaces presumably. Let me look at how live endpoints are collected in planApply.
read · completed

122: // networkModeContainerTarget returns the referenced node name for a
123: // "network-mode: container:<name>" config, or "" if networkMode does not
124: // share another container's network namespace.
125: func networkModeContainerTarget(networkMode string) string {
126: 	if target, shared := strings.CutPrefix(networkMode, "container:"); shared {
127: 		return target
128: 	}
129: 	return ""
130: }
131: 
132: // checkApplyNetworkModeTargets rejects apply when a node's network-mode:
133: // container:<target> target is being removed from the topology while the
134: // dependent node stays desired. Deleting the target would leave the
135: // dependent silently attached to a defunct network namespace.
136: func (c *CLab) checkApplyNetworkModeTargets(plan *applyPlan) error {
137: 	if plan == nil {
138: 		return nil
139: 	}
140: 
141: 	for _, nodeName := range sortedNodeNames(c.Nodes) {
142: 		networkMode := c.Nodes[nodeName].Config().NetworkMode
143: 		target := networkModeContainerTarget(networkMode)
144: 		if target == "" {
145: 			continue
146: 		}
147: 		if _, deleted := plan.deletedNodeSet[target]; deleted {
148: 			return fmt.Errorf(
149: 				"node %q has network-mode %q, but %q is being removed from the topology; "+
150: 					"remove or retarget %q as well",
151: 				nodeName,
152: 				networkMode,
153: 				target,
154: 				nodeName,
155: 			)
156: 		}
157: 	}
158: 
159: 	return nil
160: }
161: 
162: // planNetworkModeCascade forces recreation of nodes whose network-mode:
163: // container:<target> target is itself being recreated. Docker binds
164: // NetworkMode to the target container's ID at creation time, so a plain
165: // restart of the dependent cannot rebind it to the target's new container;
166: // only recreating the dependent does. A reverse dependency graph visits each
167: // node and dependency at most once, including long namespace-sharing chains.
168: func (c *CLab) planNetworkModeCascade(plan *applyPlan) {
169: 	if plan == nil || len(plan.recreatedNodeSet) == 0 {
170: 		return
171: 	}
172: 
173: 	dependents := make(map[string][]string)
174: 	for name, node := range c.Nodes {
175: 		if target := networkModeContainerTarget(node.Config().NetworkMode); target != "" {
176: 			dependents[target] = append(dependents[target], name)
177: 		}
178: 	}
179: 	queue := make([]string, 0, len(c.Nodes))
180: 	for name := range plan.recreatedNodeSet {
181: 		queue = append(queue, name)
182: 	}
183: 	for i := 0; i < len(queue); i++ {
184: 		target := queue[i]
185: 		for _, nodeName := range dependents[target] {
186: 			if _, recreated := plan.recreatedNodeSet[nodeName]; recreated {
187: 				continue
188: 			}
189: 			if _, added := plan.addedNodeSet[nodeName]; added {
190: 				continue
191: 			}
192: 
193: 			plan.recreatedNodeSet[nodeName] = struct{}{}
194: 			delete(plan.restartNodeSet, nodeName)
195: 			delete(plan.linkRestartNodeSet, nodeName)
196: 			plan.nodeChangeReasons[nodeName] = fmt.Sprintf(
197: 				"network-mode target %q recreated", target,
198: 			)
199: 			queue = append(queue, nodeName)
200: 		}
201: 	}
202: }
203: 
204: func (c *CLab) planApply(
205: 	ctx context.Context,
206: 	currentNodes map[string]*runtimeNodeGroup,
207: ) (*applyPlan, error) {
208: 	state, err := c.LoadState()
209: 	if err != nil {
210: 		log.Warn("Failed to load state file", "error", err)
211: 	}
212: 
213: 	plan := newApplyPlan(currentNodes, state)
214: 
215: 	for _, nodeName := range sortedNodeNames(c.Nodes) {
216: 		if _, exists := currentNodes[nodeName]; !exists {
217: 			status := c.Nodes[nodeName].GetContainerStatus(ctx)
218: 			if status != clabruntime.NotFound {
219: 				return nil, fmt.Errorf(
220: 					"node %q container %q already exists outside the runtime lab state",
221: 					nodeName,
222: 					c.Nodes[nodeName].Config().LongName,
223: 				)
224: 			}
225: 
226: 			plan.addedNodeSet[nodeName] = struct{}{}
227: 			continue
228: 		}
229: 
230: 		if plan.isNewNonContainerNode(nodeName) {
231: 			plan.addedNodeSet[nodeName] = struct{}{}
232: 		}
233: 	}
234: 
235: 	for _, nodeName := range sortedRuntimeNodeGroupNames(currentNodes) {
236: 		if _, exists := c.Nodes[nodeName]; !exists {
237: 			plan.deletedNodeSet[nodeName] = struct{}{}
238: 		}
239: 	}
240: 
241: 	if err := c.checkApplyNetworkModeTargets(plan); err != nil {
242: 		return nil, err
243: 	}
244: 
245: 	if err := c.planNodeReconciliation(ctx, plan); err != nil {
246: 		return nil, err
247: 	}
248: 
249: 	c.planParkedNodes(ctx, plan)
250: 	c.planStoppedNodes(ctx, plan)
251: 
252: 	for _, linkIdx := range sortedLinkIndexes(c.Links) {
253: 		for _, ep := range clablinks.RuntimeEndpoints(c.Links[linkIdx]) {
254: 			plan.desiredEndpointSet[endpointKeyFromEndpoint(ep)] = struct{}{}
255: 		}
256: 	}
257: 
258: 	if err := c.discoverLiveApplyEndpoints(ctx, plan); err != nil {
259: 		return nil, err
260: 	}
261: 
262: 	if err := c.validateDesiredEndpointOwnership(ctx, plan); err != nil {
263: 		return nil, err
264: 	}
265: 
266: 	c.planDeletedEndpoints(ctx, plan)
267: 
268: 	for _, linkIdx := range sortedLinkIndexes(c.Links) {
269: 		link := c.Links[linkIdx]
270: 		if !plan.linkNeedsDeploy(link) {
271: 			continue
272: 		}
273: 
274: 		plan.addDeployApplyLink(linkIdx, link)
275: 
276: 		for _, ep := range clablinks.RuntimeEndpoints(link) {
277: 			nodeName := ep.GetNode().GetShortName()
278: 			if _, exists := currentNodes[nodeName]; !exists {
279: 				continue
280: 			}
281: 			if _, added := plan.addedNodeSet[nodeName]; added {
282: 				continue
283: 			}
284: 
285: 			c.planAffectedApplyNode(ctx, plan, nodeName, "added link")
286: 		}
287: 	}
288: 
289: 	// Link reconciliation can request additional recreations. Propagate namespace
290: 	// dependencies only after those decisions, then park every affected live node.
291: 	c.planNetworkModeCascade(plan)
292: 	c.planTailscaleSidecarRecreates(plan)
293: 	c.planParkedNodes(ctx, plan)
294: 	c.planRecreatedNodeLinks(plan)
295: 	for nodeName := range plan.recreatedNodeSet {
296: 		delete(plan.restartNodeSet, nodeName)
297: 		delete(plan.linkRestartNodeSet, nodeName)
298: 		delete(plan.startNodeSet, nodeName)
299: 	}
300: 	if err := c.planNetworkModeRestarts(plan); err != nil {
301: 		return nil, err
302: 	}
303: 
304: 	return plan, nil
305: }
306: 
307: func (p *applyPlan) addDeployApplyLink(linkIdx int, link clablinks.Link) {
308: 	if _, planned := p.plannedLinkSet[linkIdx]; planned {
309: 		return
310: 	}
311: 
312: 	p.plannedLinkSet[linkIdx] = struct{}{}
313: 	p.addedLinks = append(p.addedLinks, link)
314: }
315: 
316: func (c *CLab) planParkedNodes(ctx context.Context, plan *applyPlan) {
317: 	if plan == nil {
318: 		return
319: 	}
320: 
321: 	for nodeName := range plan.recreatedNodeSet {
322: 		if _, parked := plan.parkedNodeSet[nodeName]; parked {
323: 			continue

335: func (c *CLab) planStoppedNodes(ctx context.Context, plan *applyPlan) {
336: 	if plan == nil {
337: 		return
338: 	}
339: 
340: 	for nodeName := range plan.currentNodes {
341: 		if plan.isNonContainerNode(nodeName) {
342: 			continue
343: 		}
344: 		node, exists := c.Nodes[nodeName]
345: 		if !exists {
346: 			continue
347: 		}
348: 		if _, deleted := plan.deletedNodeSet[nodeName]; deleted {
349: 			continue
350: 		}

352: 			continue
353: 		}

356: 		}
357:

359: 		case clabruntime.Stopped, clabruntime.Created:
360: 			plan.startNodeSet[nodeName] = struct{}{}
361: 		}
362: 	}
363: }

365: func (c *CLab) planRecreatedNodeLinks(plan *applyPlan) {
366: 	if plan == nil || len(plan.recreatedNodeSet) == 0 {
367: 		return
368: 	}

371: 	for nodeName := range plan.recreatedNodeSet {
372: 		if _, parked := plan.parkedNodeSet[nodeName]; parked {
373: 			continue
374: 		}
375: 		rebuildSet[nodeName] = struct{}{}
376: 	}
377: 
378: 	for _, linkIdx := range sortedLinkIndexes(c.Links) {
379: 		link := c.Links[linkIdx]
380: 		if !linkTouchesNodeSet(link, rebuildSet) {
381: 			continue
382: 		}
383:

385: 	}
386: }

388: func linkTouchesNodeSet(link clablinks.Link, nodeSet map[string]struct{}) bool {
389: 	if len(nodeSet) == 0 {
390: 		return false
391: 	}
392:

395: 		if _, exists := nodeSet[key.node]; exists {
396: 			return true
397: 		}
398: 	}
399: 
400: 	return false
401: }
402: 
403: func (c *CLab) planDeletedEndpoints(ctx context.Context, plan *applyPlan) {
404: 	plannedEndpointSet := map[applyEndpointKey]struct{}{}

406: 	for _, key := range sortedEndpointKeys(plan.liveEndpointSet) {

408: 			continue
409: 		}
410: 		c.addStaleApplyEndpoint(ctx, plan, plannedEndpointSet, key)
411: 	}
412: }

map[string]string\n}\n\nfunc (p *applyPlan) empty() bool {\n\treturn len(p.addedNodeSet) == 0 &&\n\t\tlen(p.deletedNodeSet) == 0 &&\n\t\tlen(p.recreatedNodeSet) == 0 &&\n\t\tlen(p.startNodeSet) == 0 host,\n// ext-container) is absent from the previously deployed topology. Such nodes\n// exist outside the lab, so they are always listed as current nodes, even\n// when the topology has just added them.\nfunc (p *applyPlan) isNewNonContainerNode(nodeName string) bool {\n\tif !p.isNonContainerNode(nodeName) || p.state == nil || p.state.Topology == nil {\n\t\treturn false\n\t}\n\t_, exists := p.state.Topology.Nodes[nodeName]\n\treturn !exists\n}\n\n// networkModeContainerTarget returns the referenced node name for a\n// \"network-mode: container:<name>\" config, or \"\" if networkMode does not\n// share another container's network namespace.\nfunc networkModeContainerTarget(networkMode string) string {\n\tif target, shared := strings.CutPrefix(networkMode, \"container:\"); shared {\n\t\treturn target\n\t}\n\treturn \"\"\n}\n\n// checkApplyNetworkModeTargets rejects apply when a node's network-mode:\n// container:<target> target is being removed from the topology while the\n// dependent node stays desired. Deleting the target would leave the\n// dependent silently attached to a defunct network namespace.\nfunc (c *CLab) checkApplyNetworkModeTargets(plan *applyPlan) error {\n\tif plan == nil {\n\t\treturn nil\n\t}\n\n\tfor _, nodeName := range sortedNodeNames(c.Nodes) {\n\t\tnetworkMode := c.Nodes[nodeName].Config().NetworkMode\n\t\ttarget := networkModeContainerTarget(networkMode)\n\t\tif target == \"\" {\n\t\t\tcontinue\n\t\t}\n\t\tif _, deleted := plan.deletedNodeSet[target]; deleted {\n\t\t\treturn fmt.Errorf(\n\t\t\t\t\"node %q has network-mode %q, but %q is being removed from the topology; \"+\n\t\t\t\t\t\"remove or retarget %q as well\",\n\t\t\t\tnodeName,\n\t\t\t\tnetworkMode,\n\t\t\t\ttarget,\n\t\t\t\tnodeName,\n\t\t\t)\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\n\treturn nil\n}\n\n// planNetworkModeCascade forces recreation of nodes whose network-mode:\n// container:<target> target is itself being recreated. Docker binds\n// NetworkMode to the target container's ID at creation time, so a plain\n// restart of the dependent cannot rebind it to the target's new container;\n// only recreating the dependent does. A reverse dependency graph visits each\n// node and dependency at most once, including long namespace-sharing chains.\nfunc (c *CLab) planNetworkModeCascade(plan *applyPlan) {\n\tif plan == nil || len(plan.recreatedNodeSet) == 0 {\n\t\treturn\n\t}\n\n\tdependents := make(map[string][]string)\n\tfor name, node := range c.Nodes {\n\t\tif target := networkModeContainerTarget(node.Config().NetworkMode); target != \"\" {\n\t\t\tdependents[target] = append(dependents[target], name)\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\tqueue := make([]string, 0, len(c.Nodes))\n\tfor name := range plan.recreatedNodeSet {\n\t\tqueue = append(queue, name)\n\t}\n\tfor i := 0; i < len(queue); i++ {\n\t\ttarget := queue[i]\n\t\tfor _, nodeName := range dependents[target] {\n\t\t\tif _, recreated := plan.recreatedNodeSet[nodeName]; recreated {\n\t\t\t\tcontinue\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t\tif _, added := plan.addedNodeSet[nodeName]; added {\n\t\t\t\tcontinue\n\t\t\t}\n\n\t\t\tplan.recreatedNodeSet[nodeName] = struct{}{}\n\t\t\tdelete(plan.restartNodeSet, nodeName)\n\t\t\tdelete(plan.linkRestartNodeSet, nodeName)\n\t\t\tplan.nodeChangeReasons[nodeName] = fmt.Sprintf(\n\t\t\t\t\"network-mode target %q recreated\", target,\n\t\t\t)\n\t\t\tqueue = append(queue, nodeName)\n\t\t}\n\t}\n}\n\nfunc (c *CLab) planApply(\n\tctx context.Context,\n\tcurrentNodes map[string]*runtimeNodeGroup,\n) (*applyPlan, error) {\n\tstate, err := c.LoadState()\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\tlog.Warn(\"Failed to load state file\", \"error\", err)\n\t}\n\n\tplan := newApplyPlan(currentNodes, state)\n\n\tfor _, nodeName := range sortedNodeNames(c.Nodes) {\n\t\tif _, exists := currentNodes[nodeName]; !exists {\n\t\t\tstatus := c.Nodes[nodeName].GetContainerStatus(ctx)\n\t\t\tif status != clabruntime.NotFound {\n\t\t\t\treturn nil, fmt.Errorf(\n\t\t\t\t\t\"node %q container %q already exists outside the runtime lab state\",\n\t\t\t\t\tnodeName,\n\t\t\t\t\tc.Nodes[nodeName].Config().LongName,\n\t\t\t\t)\n\t\t\t}\n\n\t\t\tplan.addedNodeSet[nodeName] = struct{}{}\n\t\t\tcontinue\n\t\t}\n\n\t\tif plan.isNewNonContainerNode(nodeName) {\n\t\t\tplan.addedNodeSet[nodeName] = struct{}{}\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\n\tfor _, nodeName := range sortedRuntimeNodeGroupNames(currentNodes) {\n\t\tif _, exists := c.Nodes[nodeName]; !exists {\n\t\t\tplan.deletedNodeSet[nodeName] = struct{}{}\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\n\tif err := c.checkApplyNetworkModeTargets(plan); err != nil {\n\t\treturn nil, err\n\t}\n\n\tif err := c.planNodeReconciliation(ctx, plan); err != nil {\n\t\treturn nil, err\n\t}\n\n\tc.planParkedNodes(ctx, plan)\n\tc.planStoppedNodes(ctx, plan)\n\n\tfor _, linkIdx := range sortedLinkIndexes(c.Links) {\n\t\tfor _, ep := range clablinks.RuntimeEndpoints(c.Links[linkIdx]) {\n\t\t\tplan.desiredEndpointSet[endpointKeyFromEndpoint(ep)] = struct{}{}\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\n\tif err := c.discoverLiveApplyEndpoints(ctx, plan); err != nil {\n\t\treturn nil, err\n\t}\n\n\tif err := c.validateDesiredEndpointOwnership(ctx, plan); err != nil {\n\t\treturn nil, err\n\t}\n\n\tc.planDeletedEndpoints(ctx, plan)\n\n\tfor _, linkIdx := range sortedLinkIndexes(c.Links) {\n\t\tlink := c.Links[linkIdx]\n\t\tif !plan.linkNeedsDeploy(link) {\n\t\t\tcontinue\n\t\t}\n\n\t\tplan.addDeployApplyLink(linkIdx, link)\n\n\t\tfor _, ep := range clablinks.RuntimeEndpoints(link) {\n\t\t\tnodeName := ep.GetNode().GetShortName()\n\t\t\tif _, exists := currentNodes[nodeName]; Propagate namespace\n\t// dependencies only after those decisions, then park every affected live node.\n\tc.planNetworkModeCascade(plan)\n\tc.planTailscaleSidecarRecreates(plan)\n\tc.planParkedNodes(ctx, plan)\n\tc.planRecreatedNodeLinks(plan)\n\tfor nodeName := range plan.recreatedNodeSet {\n\t\tdelete(plan.restartNodeSet, nodeName)\n\t\tdelete(plan.linkRestartNodeSet, nodeName)\n\t\tdelete(plan.startNodeSet, nodeName)\n\t}\n\tif err := c.planNetworkModeRestarts(plan); Reasoning
Summarize the review task state.
## Objective
- Perform a source-only PR review of a read-only snapshot at `/source` (containerlab repo, head `8f46e35d0b07ce23894604a8eef0322e82034a61`, base `52f8203cd949f63caea2f479fce024bab1881e62`) covering all 29 changed paths, then return the trusted result schema. The PR adds multi-management-network support: mgmt-net links can now select a management network by name via a `network` key.

## Important Details
- Snapshot is read-only; source-only review without executing tests is acceptable but must disclose that limitation.
- Initial `read /source` was blocked by permission rules; read files via explicit paths (`/source/...`, `/source/head/...`, `/source/base/...`) instead.
- Standards referenced by `standards.json`: `.cursor/rules/karpathy-guidelines.mdc` (surgical changes, simplicity, surface assumptions) and `.cursor/rules/ponytail.mdc` (simplest solution, root-cause fixes, grep all callers).
- PR mechanism: `LinkMgmtNetRaw` gains `Network string` yaml `network,omitempty`; `links.ResolveParams` gains `DefaultMgmtNetwork`; `getMgmtBrLinkNode(network)` now keys a mutex-guarded package map `_mgmtBrLinkNodes map[string]*mgmtBridgeLinkNode` (empty name = default network); stable owner name `mgmtNetOwnerName = "mgmt-net"` replaces per-bridge altname markers (with legacy altname acceptance via `isOwnedBy`).
- New constants: `DefaultMgmtNetworkName = "clab"` in `constants/containerlab.go` (clabernetes fallback).
- New core flow: `apply` now calls `c.resolveMgmtNetLinkNetworks(ctx)` then `c.setMgmtNetLinkBridges()` (replacing `clablinks.SetMgmtNetUnderlyingBridge(c.Config.Mgmt.Bridge)`); `deploy` flow: `ResolveLinks()` (deploy.go:190) → `prepareLabManagementNetwork` (206) → `checkTopologyDefinition` (211); `prepareLabManagementNetwork` = CreateNetwork → allocate IPs → setMgmtNetLinkBridges.
- Ordering analysis so far: node shortname is read at deploy time (`AddLinkToContainer` → `netlink.LinkByName(b.shortname)`), so resolving links before bridges are set is safe; unresolved bridges (`""` or `"mgmt-net"`) skip bridge checks via `IsUnresolvedMgmtBridgeEndpoint` and are re-set after network creation.
- Runtimes: docker and podman implement `MgmtNetBinder`/`MgmtBridgeResolver` (`ForMgmtNet`, `ResolveMgmtBridge` writing through `*MgmtNet` pointer); runtimes without binder get "runtime %q does not support multiple management networks" error; tests mock these (`mocks/mockruntime` MockMgmtNetBinder).
- Pointer-identity concern: `m == c.Config.Mgmt` is used to skip the default network — need to confirm topology load puts the same `*MgmtNet` pointer into `Config.MgmtNetworks[0]`.
- Legacy compat concern: `isOwnedBy` legacy acceptance checks `HasOwnershipAltNameFor(link, node.GetShortName(), iface)` with the *current* bridge; a lab deployed by an older release whose mgmt-net link moved to a different network (bridge changed) may fail `RemoveOwnedInterface` cleanup with interface considered unowned — likely P3 (pre-existing issue, slightly worsened by multi-network).
- Noted minor inconsistency: clabernetes brief-link check accepts `network == "clab"` when `mgmt.Network == ""`, but `Resolve` maps only exact `params.DefaultMgmtNetwork` matches to "".
- Removed `SetMgmtNetUnderlyingBridge` calls from `exec.go` and `save.go` (likely safe; not fully verified).
- `GetMgmtBrLinkNodes` calls `getMgmtBrLinkNode("")` (may create default node) then re-locks to iterate — benign race.

I have next steps. Continue the review: I need to finish tracing. Key remaining checks:

1. `discoverLiveApplyEndpoints` — how live endpoints are discovered for "mgmt-net" node (uses applyEndpointDiscoveryNodes which includes only default mgmt node; but discovery uses ownershipName = mgmtNetOwnerName for all mgmt nodes, so interfaces owned by other networks' bridges should still be discovered IF discovery happens via the default node's nspath and owner name "mgmt-net"). Wait — DiscoverOwnedInterfacesFor(ctx, node, ownerName) — need to check whether discovery uses ownershipName(node) or passed owner name, and whether the discovery of live mgmt-net endpoints is done via the default node. If the live mgmt-net interface belongs to a non-default network, its altname is ownershipAltNameFor("mgmt-net", iface) after this PR's deployment. Discovery with the default mgmt node: node's nspath = host ns; ownershipName(node) = mgmtNetOwnerName ("mgmt-net") regardless of shortname. So all mgmt-net-owned interfaces are discovered. 

But legacy interfaces deployed by older release have altname ownershipAltNameFor(bridgeName, iface) where bridgeName is the current bridge of that interface's network. Discovery with default node would only match legacy altname if bridgeName == default node's shortname. So a legacy lab with multiple... no wait, legacy labs only have ONE mgmt network (the default). Legacy interfaces only exist on the default bridge. After upgrade, if the default network's bridge stays the same, fine. If the default network's bridge changed (e.g., mgmt.Network name changed between deployments... actually bridge is derived from network name, stable), or if the user moved an existing link to a new network name — then the old interface on the default bridge has legacy altname "br-old:iface" — hmm but that interface is not part of the new topology anymore, so it would be discovered? isOwnedBy with default node's shortname — if the old bridge differs from the new default bridge, discovery won't claim it, and it stays orphaned. That's the pre-existing edge noted. Actually wait: with this PR, `isOwnedBy` legacy acceptance checks the CURRENT node shortname. Let me re-read ownership.go to confirm.

12: 
13: var ErrInterfaceUnowned = errors.New("interface is not marked as containerlab-owned")
14: 
15: // OwnedInterface describes a containerlab-owned interface discovered in a node's
16: // network namespace.
17: type OwnedInterface struct {
18: 	Name        string
19: 	Index       int
20: 	PeerIndex   int
21: 	MasterIndex int
22: 	MasterName  string
23: 	Type        string
24: }
25: 
26: // DiscoverOwnedInterfaces returns containerlab-owned interfaces and the
27: // netlink metadata needed to verify veth link pairing.
28: func DiscoverOwnedInterfaces(
29: 	ctx context.Context,
30: 	node Node,
31: 	knownIfaceNames map[string]struct{},
32: ) ([]OwnedInterface, error) {
33: 	return DiscoverOwnedInterfacesFor(ctx, node, ownershipName(node), knownIfaceNames)
34: }
35: 
36: // DiscoverOwnedInterfacesFor discovers interfaces in node's namespace that
37: // carry ownership markers for ownerName. Parking nodes use this when the
38: // namespace and logical node differ.
39: func DiscoverOwnedInterfacesFor(
40: 	ctx context.Context,
41: 	node Node,
42: 	ownerName string,
43: 	knownIfaceNames map[string]struct{},
44: ) ([]OwnedInterface, error) {
45: 	var interfaces []OwnedInterface
46: 
47: 	err := node.ExecFunction(ctx, func(_ ns.NetNS) error {
48: 		links, err := netlink.LinkList()
49: 		if err != nil {
50: 			return err
51: 		}
52: 
53: 		for _, link := range links {
54: 			name := link.Attrs().Name
55: 			if name == "lo" || name == "eth0" {
56: 				continue
57: 			}
58: 			if knownIfaceNames != nil {
59: 				if _, known := knownIfaceNames[name]; !known {
60: 					continue
61: 				}
62: 			}
63: 			if !isOwnedBy(link, node, ownerName, name) {
64: 				continue
65: 			}
66: 
67: 			info := OwnedInterface{
68: 				Name:        name,
69: 				Index:       link.Attrs().Index,
70: 				MasterIndex: link.Attrs().MasterIndex,
71: 				Type:        link.Type(),
72: 			}
73: 			if info.MasterIndex != 0 {
74: 				master, masterErr := netlink.LinkByIndex(info.MasterIndex)
75: 				if masterErr != nil {
76: 					return fmt.Errorf(
77: 						"failed to discover master for %q: %w",
78: 						name,
79: 						masterErr,
80: 					)
81: 				}
82: 				info.MasterName = master.Attrs().Name
83: 			}
84: 			if info.Type == "veth" {
85: 				veth, ok := link.(*netlink.Veth)
86: 				if !ok {
87: 					veth = &netlink.Veth{LinkAttrs: *link.Attrs()}
88: 				}
89: 				peerIndex, peerErr := netlink.VethPeerIndex(veth)
90: 				if peerErr != nil {
91: 					return fmt.Errorf("failed to discover veth peer for %q: %w", name, peerErr)
92: 				}
93: 				info.PeerIndex = peerIndex
94: 			}
95: 
96: 			interfaces = append(interfaces, info)
97: 		}
98: 
99: 		return nil
100: 	})
101: 	if err != nil {
102: 		return nil, err
103: 	}
104: 
105: 	sort.Slice(interfaces, func(i, j int) bool {
106: 		return interfaces[i].Name < interfaces[j].Name
107: 	})
108: 
109: 	return interfaces, nil
110: }
111: 
112: func DiscoverOwnedInterfaceNames(
113: 	ctx context.Context,
114: 	node Node,
115: 	knownIfaceNames map[string]struct{},
116: ) ([]string, error) {
117: 	interfaces, err := DiscoverOwnedInterfaces(ctx, node, knownIfaceNames)
118: 	if err != nil {
119: 		return nil, err
120: 	}
121: 
122: 	ifaceNames := make([]string, 0, len(interfaces))
123: 	for _, iface := range interfaces {
124: 		ifaceNames = append(ifaceNames, iface.Name)
125: 	}
126: 	sort.Strings(ifaceNames)
127: 
128: 	return ifaceNames, nil
129: }
130: 
131: // ValidateOwnedInterface returns an error when an existing interface is not
132: // owned by the requested logical endpoint. Missing interfaces are valid.
133: func ValidateOwnedInterface(ctx context.Context, node Node, ifaceName string) error {
134: 	return ValidateOwnedInterfaceFor(ctx, node, ownershipName(node), ifaceName)
135: }
136: 
137: // ValidateOwnedInterfaceFor validates an interface in node's namespace against
138: // a possibly different logical owner. Parking nodes use this when the
139: // interface remains owned by the original node.
140: func ValidateOwnedInterfaceFor(
141: 	ctx context.Context,
142: 	node Node,
143: 	ownerName string,
144: 	ifaceName string,
145: ) error {
146: 	return node.ExecFunction(ctx, func(_ ns.NetNS) error {
147: 		link, err := netlink.LinkByName(ifaceName)
148: 		if _, notfound := err.(netlink.LinkNotFoundError); notfound {
149: 			return nil
150: 		}
151: 		if err != nil {
152: 			return err
153: 		}
154: 		if isOwnedBy(link, node, ownerName, ifaceName) {
155: 			return nil
156: 		}
157: 		return fmt.Errorf(
158: 			"interface %q on node %q exists but is not containerlab-owned",
159: 			ifaceName,
160: 			node.GetShortName(),
161: 		)
162: 	})
163: }
164: 
165: func RemoveOwnedInterface(ctx context.Context, node Node, ifaceName string) error {
166: 	if node == nil || ifaceName == "" {
167: 		return nil
168: 	}
169: 
170: 	return node.ExecFunction(ctx, func(_ ns.NetNS) error {
171: 		link, err := netlink.LinkByName(ifaceName)
172: 		if _, notfound := err.(netlink.LinkNotFoundError); notfound {
173: 			return nil
174: 		}
175: 		if err != nil {
176: 			return err
177: 		}
178: 		if !isOwnedBy(link, node, ownershipName(node), ifaceName) {
179: 			return fmt.Errorf(
180: 				"%w: interface %q on node %q",
181: 				ErrInterfaceUnowned,
182: 				ifaceName,
183: 				node.GetShortName(),
184: 			)
185: 		}
186: 
187: 		return netlink.LinkDel(link)
188: 	})
189: }
190: 
191: // isOwnedBy reports whether link carries the ownership marker of the logical
192: // endpoint ownerName:ifaceName. Interfaces of mgmt-net links deployed by earlier
193: // releases carry a marker for the bridge name of the mgmt-net link node instead
194: // of the stable mgmt-net owner name, and are accepted as well.
195: func isOwnedBy(link netlink.Link, node Node, ownerName, ifaceName string) bool {
196: 	if HasOwnershipAltNameFor(link, ownerName, ifaceName) {
197: 		return true
198: 	}
199: 
200: 	if _, ok := node.(*mgmtBridgeLinkNode); ok && ownerName == mgmtNetOwnerName {
201: 		return HasOwnershipAltNameFor(link, node.GetShortName(), ifaceName)
202: 	}
203: 
204: 	return false
205: }
